I’d be up before daylight loading tools, drawing out flower beds or landscaping plans, pricing native plants, and then after working outside all day I’d still feel like I had to keep going. Before I crashed, I felt really fired up, like almost bigger than myself. And if one of the guys questioned my plan or told me I should slow down, I’d snap at him way harder than made sense. It didn’t feel like my normal self.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a pattern where these periods of high energy and activity are followed by periods of depression, and then the cycle repeats?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about a clear cycle like that. I know I’ve had the high-energy stretches, and then I’ve had times where I feel really low afterward, but I haven’t tracked it enough to say it repeats in a pattern.\n\nDoctor: How long do your periods of feeling depressed usually last?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on a long time this time. I’d say the depression’s been hanging over me for about a year, and this latest crash is probably the worst it’s gotten.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel really down or depressed after one of these high-energy periods?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Before I crashed, there was a stretch where I felt really fired up, almost like I was bigger than myself. Before I crashed, I felt really fired up, like almost bigger than myself. Not just happy exactly, more like I was sure I could make it all happen. But I was also on edge—if a coworker questioned my plan or told me to slow down, I’d snap at him way harder than made sense.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience a period of feeling sad, empty, or depressed after you crashed?\n\nPatient: Yeah. After I crashed, I felt really depressed. And honestly, it hasn’t just been a few days — it’s been hanging over me for about a year now. This latest crash is the worst it’s gotten.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any periods where you didn't need much sleep, yet you still had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t remember it clearly as “not needing sleep” so much. I know I was wound up and busy, but I couldn’t say for sure how much I was sleeping or whether I had more energy despite it.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel unusually important or superior to others during the time you were working on your park idea?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I did. I really thought I was about to be the guy every town around here would call for sustainable landscaping. Like I was ahead of everybody, even though in reality I’m still just trying to keep my small jobs paid up.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever hear voices or see things that weren't really there during these periods of high energy or after you crashed?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t heard voices or seen things that weren’t there. It’s been more like my mood and thoughts getting really intense, but not that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had other times like this in the past, where you felt really energized and were taking on a lot of projects?\n\nPatient: Yeah, there have been other times like that. I’d be up before daylight loading tools, drawing out flower beds or landscaping plans, pricing native plants, and then after working outside all day I’d still feel like I had to keep going. Like I couldn’t just stop and rest.\n\nDoctor: Did you have a period where you felt extremely high or irritable and your thoughts were racing, separate from your normal personality?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Before I crashed, I felt really fired up, like almost bigger than myself. And if one of the guys questioned my plan or told me I should slow down, I’d snap at him way harder than made sense. It didn’t feel like my normal self.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a pattern where these periods of high energy and activity are followed by periods of depression, and then the cycle repeats?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about a clear cycle like that. I know I’ve had the high-energy stretches, and then I’ve had times where I feel really low afterward, but I haven’t tracked it enough to say it repeats in a pattern.\n\nDoctor: How long do your periods of feeling depressed usually last?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on a long time this time. I’d say the depression’s been hanging over me for about a year, and this latest crash is probably the worst it’s gotten.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel really down or depressed after one of these high-energy periods?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. This time it’s like the depression’s been hanging over me for about a year, and this latest crash is the worst it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely high or irritable that lasted for several days or more?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Before I crashed, there was a stretch where I felt really fired up, almost like I was bigger than myself. That last time, when I was trying to remake myself for that park pitch, I was really fired up and overconfident for about two weeks before I crashed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely high or irritable that lasted for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least one week?\n\nPatient: Yes. Before I crashed, it was like that most days—I felt really fired up, almost bigger than myself. And I was irritable too. If a coworker questioned my plan or told me to slow down, I’d snap at him way harder than made sense.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel so high or irritable during one of these energetic periods that you did things that had serious consequences, like spending a lot of money or getting into trouble?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I did. I started putting a bunch of stuff on a credit card like it was all going to pay for itself—tools, plants, work clothes, even ads. At the time it felt like the money was basically already coming in. Now I look at the balance and I feel sick, because I can’t cover it.\n\nDoctor: How long do your periods of feeling depressed usually last after one of these high-energy periods?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on a long time. The depressed part has been hanging over me for about a year now, and this latest crash after being so fired up is the worst it’s gotten.\n\nDoctor: How many days would you say you typically feel really fired up and energetic during one of these periods?\n\nPatient: Usually about two weeks. That last time, when I started trying to remake myself for the park pitch, I was really fired up and overconfident for maybe fourteen days before I crashed.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any periods of feeling extremely high or irritable that lasted for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah. And if someone at work questioned what I was doing or told me to slow down, I’d snap at them way harder than I should have.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever have a manic episode that required hospitalization?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never been hospitalized for anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
